# SD-31 — Open Issues, Blockers, and Operator Rulings Needed

**How this file works.** SD-31 runs unattended. A cycle never stops to ask a question. When a cycle
hits a hard block, needs an operator ruling, or takes a default it wants reviewed, it appends a row
here and keeps working. The operator reads this file on check-in and answers in the `Operator ruling`
column.

**Rules for cycles appending here:**
- Append, never rewrite another entry.
- One row per issue. Give the exact command and exit code, not a narration.
- `Severity`: `BLOCKER` (work stopped on this card), `RULING-NEEDED` (proceeded on a default, operator
  should confirm), `NOTE` (recorded, no action wanted).
- A Structural Exclusion Register proposal (`decisions.md §3`) is logged here as `RULING-NEEDED` with
  a pointer to the proposal — a cycle may propose, only the operator grants.

**THE LIVE ONE, added 2026-08-17 by `SD31-W9-INTEGRATE-001`: race attribution — row 140 below.**
In plain terms: the 7 Core Rulebook races (Dwarf, Elf, Gnome, Half-Elf, Half-Orc, Halfling, Human)
and 9 Bestiary 4 races currently sit on the board under a real, independently-published Paizo
sourcebook (Inner Sea Races, 2015) purely because it is the newest book that CITES their base
traits — even though it never originally PUBLISHED any of them and carries zero of its own base
race declarations (it is 100% `.MOD` citation rows over races other books actually declare). The
question: does a citation-only compendium count as a "printing" under Decision 10's "newest
publish wins" (which would move these races' attribution to Inner Sea Races), or does it stay
excluded the same way `core_essentials` — a PCGen-internal packaging directory, not a real book —
is already excluded under Decision 9 (which would leave them at Core Rulebook / Bestiary 4)? This
is NOT the same question as `core_essentials` (Decision 9 already answered that one: not a book,
excluded); Inner Sea Races is a real, purchasable Paizo sourcebook, so Decision 9's answer does
not mechanically extend to it. Wave 8's `SD31-ATTRIB-003` built the full per-race citation evidence
table this ruling should be made from — `artifacts/SD31-ATTRIB-003-race-evidence.md` §1-§2 — showing
every affected race's citation evidence both including and excluding Inner Sea Races. Full technical
detail at row 140.

**Rows 69/87/95/107 — ANSWERED 2026-08-16, `decisions.md` §7 REFINED (`SD31-D7-PROSE-004`).** The
flat-vs-scaling question this section used to lead with was the WRONG axis; the operator's actual
ruling is UNIVERSAL vs CONDITIONAL: *"+1 size bonus to AC means you need to give a +1 on the AC -
that's computed... if that +4 bonus was ONLY against certain creature types, like with dwarf racial
hatred - that's not a universal bump... if it says acid damage - that's a condition, many things
shrug off acid."* `SD31-D7-PROSE-004` built the real discriminator
(`closure_states_universal_sheet_modifier`, `v06_work_inventory.rs`), retired the four hand-picked
`*_FLAT_MAGNITUDE_PENDING_RULING` name-lists rows 69/95/107 (and wave 7's `CLASS_FEATURE_...`/
`FEAT_...` lists) had built pending this exact ruling, and applied it corpus-wide. Outcome, both
directions, every unit individually re-derived (not estimated): **21 units promoted** (13
`class_feature` + 7 `feat` + 1 `monster_ability` — every one of the previously-parked units, all
genuinely conditional) and **6 units demoted** (`gnome_size`/`grippli_size`/`halfling_size`/
`kobold_size`/`svirfneblin_size`/`goblin_size`, the Small-race size traits — universal AC/attack/
CMB/CMD/Stealth bonuses that must compute, per the ruling's own worked example). Board: 9,780 → 9,795
done (25.3887% → 25.4277%). Full citations, the discriminator's 32-case hand-labelled accuracy proof,
and the corpus-wide false-positive fix (narrowing `UNIVERSAL_MODIFIER_CUES` to `"size bonus"` alone
after 5 false positives surfaced running the retired-list replacement against the FULL corpus) are in
`v06_work_inventory.rs`'s own doc comments and this cycle's `progress.md` receipt. **Row 87's own
`goblin_size` classification was WRONG in this cycle's own dispatch brief** ("grants nothing") — the
real shipped `bestiary:race_trait:goblin_size` row states the identical universal size-bonus shape as
the other 5 and is demoted alongside them; `retro.py correction` emitted, verified by direct `cat` of
`data/corpus/beastiary/race_trait/goblin/goblin_size.json`.

| 1 | 2026-08-15 | SD31-E2-F1-001 | NOTE | `wiring_class::CorpusLines::line()` (`src/rules_core/wiring_class.rs:758`) resolves a unit's corpus row via a **single-level** `dir.join(file)` join. Several books' `.lst` files live in nested subdirectories (`core_essentials/races/<race>/*.lst`, `ultimate_combat/support/*.lst`, `horror_adventures/support/*.lst`, `inner_sea_world_guide/_pfs/*.lst`, `advanced_race_guide/_pfs/*.lst`, `adventurers_guide/support/*.lst`, etc.) — the join silently misses, `CorpusLines::line()` returns `None`, and the unit falls into `ambiguous:no_corpus_line` (D0) even though its row genuinely exists. Re-derived corpus-wide: `python3 -c "import json; d=json.load(open('docs/work-inventory.json')); u=[x for x in d['units'] if x.get('book')!='beginner_box']; ncl=[x for x in u if x.get('wiring_class_reason')=='no_corpus_line']; print(len(ncl))"` → **1,707** units (was documented as 47 in `docs/release/GE-01-legacy-corpus-and-conversion-matrix/artifacts/wiring-class-determination.md`, now stale). Recursive-glob check confirms **100% (1,707/1,707)** of these rows are findable somewhere under the book directory — none are genuinely synthetic/provenance-free. This is **80.9% of the whole 2,109-unit `ambiguous` population**. Not a blocker to SD31-E2-F1 (the ground-truth sample hand-resolved all 40 affected sampled units from the real row instead), but it is load-bearing for Epic 2-F2 (classifier build) and F3 (`ambiguous` dead-end closure): fixing the path join before building/accepting a classifier will move the vast majority of "ambiguous" units to their real class for free, and F3's "genuinely unreachable, propose to Structural Exclusion Register" review must not run against the current, badly-inflated `no_corpus_line` bucket. | Recorded the bug with full re-derivation and worked examples in `artifacts/SD31-E2-F1-ground-truth-methodology.md` §Finding A; hand-labelled the 40 affected sampled units from their real corpus rows (not the engine's `ambiguous` verdict) so the ground-truth sample itself is not corrupted by the same bug. No production code changed (out of this card's scope). | *(needs operator/Epic-2-F2 owner confirmation before any fix lands)* |
| 2 | 2026-08-15 | SD31-E2-F1-001 | NOTE | `wiring_class::signals()`'s scalar/arithmetic scan (`src/rules_core/wiring_class.rs:398-420`) reads the WHOLE field text, not just the magnitude value. Two consequences, both false positives for `derived`: (a) `BONUS:STAT|<ABILITY>|<literal>` fields always contain the ability's own name as the STAT selector (e.g. `STR`,`DEX`,`INT`), which collides with `SCALARS_WORD` regardless of whether the *value* is scalar-dependent — a flat `BONUS:STAT|DEX|2|TYPE=Racial` (`core_rulebook:race_trait:2_dexterity`) or `BONUS:STAT|INT|2|TYPE=Enhancement` (`ultimate_equipment:equipment:staff_of_mithral_might`) is misclassified `derived` instead of `static`. (b) `has_arith`'s unconditional `value.contains('/')` check fires on PCGen's literal `DR:10/Cold Iron`/`CR:1/3` bypass-type notation, not just division (`bestiary:monster:neothelid` compounds both). Confirmed in 3 of 150 sampled units as a clean single-cause misclassification (others carrying the same false-positive signal, e.g. `core_essentials:companion:pig`, still land on the correct class because a genuinely separate formula also fires). Full worked examples in the methodology note §Finding B. | Hand-labelled the 3 affected units `static` with the false-positive mechanism documented per-unit in the ground-truth JSON's `token_evidence` field. | *(informational — feed into Epic 2-F2's classifier acceptance work, not a blocker)* |

| 22 | 2026-08-15 | SD31-E6-F4-001 | RULING-NEEDED | **Root cause of `race` kind's 0.0 % done, traced one unit deep before any ingest work started.** The 7 CRB races (`core_essentials:race:dwarf` etc.) are `wiring_class=static`, `status=grounded`, `evidence="race_modelled_by_RaceId_ALL_and_reachable_in_a_real_receipt"` — genuinely reachable in a real receipt, one rung short of `done` only because `static`+`grounded` needs `literal-verified` (`corpus_literal_sweep`) to clear the last bar. `corpus_literal_sweep`'s own `--json-out` writer (`src/bin/corpus_literal_sweep.rs:267-276`) derives each verified triple's `"book"` field as `source_path.parent().file_name()` — the file's IMMEDIATE parent directory — not the 4-segment `book_dir_of()` grouping the rest of the binary uses. For a race chassis/trait row, the real PCGen path is `.../core_essentials/races/<race>/<race>_races.lst` (one directory level deeper than a flat book layout), so the emitted `"book"` is the RACE NAME (`"dwarf"`, `"aasimar"`, …), never `"core_essentials"`. `v06_work_inventory.rs`'s `apply_done_rung_stamps` (`src/bin/v06_work_inventory.rs:3772-3803`) joins `sweep_verified` on `(item.unit.book, file, line)`, so the join key never matches for ANY race chassis/trait row, and `literal-verified` can never stamp — regardless of how many races get a real chassis. Corroborated two ways: (a) direct: 0 of the CRB 7 races' `(book="core_essentials", file, line)` triples appear anywhere in the sweep's `verified` array (`grep`-equivalent: `python3 -c "..." ` over the `--json-out` report, 0 hits for `book=="core_essentials"`); (b) contrastive: `race_trait`'s own 5 `static`+`grounded` (held) rows are ALL under the identical nested `core_essentials/races/aasimar/aasimar_abilities_race.lst` shape, while the 4 `race_trait` units that DID reach `literal-verified` (`world_walker_skilled`, `deep_jungle_halfling_poison_use`, `junk_tinker_skilled`, `oversized_goblin_ability_scores`) are ALL filed FLAT, one level under their book (`advanced_race_guide/arg_abilities_race.lst`, etc.) — the shape, not the content, is what gates. Small, mechanical, well-understood fix; not this card's file territory.)* |

| 80 | 2026-08-16 | SD31-E4-F1-001 | NOTE | **`class_feature_owner`'s suffix-only class match produces a genuine cross-archetype false positive, discovered by this cycle's own regen, not fabricated to demonstrate a hazard.** `v06_work_inventory.rs::class_feature_owner` matches a record's group prefix against a modelled class via `group == class \|\| group.starts_with(class+" ") \|\| group.ends_with(" "+class)`. `"Stygian Slayer"` (an ACG Slayer archetype's OWN group name) ends with `" slayer"`, so it resolves `owner = "slayer"` — which is CORRECT here (Stygian Slayer really is a Slayer archetype) and is why `Stygian Slayer ~ Weapon and Armor Proficiency` correctly flips to `grounded` alongside the base `Slayer ~ Weapon and Armor Proficiency` record once my shared explanation id exists (both verified via the guarded regen this cycle, `docs/work-inventory.json` restored after). But the SAME suffix rule also matches `"Skulking Slayer"` — a **Rogue** archetype from Advanced Race Guide, corpus-unrelated to the ACG Slayer class — which ALSO now reads `owner = "slayer"` and its own `"Skulking Slayer ~ Weapon and Armor Proficiency"` record ALSO flips to `grounded`, purely because both archetypes happen to end in the word "Slayer" and happen to share a feature named "Weapon and Armor Proficiency". Confirmed via the same regen diff: `advanced_race_guide:class_feature:skulking_slayer_weapon_and_armor_proficiency` moved `not-started → held` alongside my 2 intended units. This is a **name collision, not a book/subject check** — `class_feature_owner` has no way to see that "Skulking Slayer" is a Rogue thing, not a Slayer thing, because it only ever looks at the group-name STRING. Low material impact today (both land on `held`, not `done`, per row 70; and Skulking Slayer's OWN feature text still would not be quoted, since my code only fires for the character's actually-selected ACG Slayer archetype) but a real, verified classifier hazard the next `class_feature` cycle should know about before wiring a class whose name is a common English-word suffix of another class/archetype's name. | Documented and reproduced precisely; did not attempt a fix (`class_feature_owner` is `v06_work_inventory.rs`, out of this card's file territory, and a real fix needs book-scoping the match, not a quick patch). | *(informational — no ruling needed; worth a `class_feature_owner` book-scoping enhancement in a future `v06_work_inventory.rs` cycle so archetype-name suffix collisions across unrelated classes cannot silently share credit)* |

Raised by the OPERATOR from the dashboard, not by any instrument:** *"race is at 0%, I don't see the core rules book listed under race, and advanced race guide reports as nearly untouched — we spent a lot of time on both."* All three observations are correct and share one cause. `src/rules_core/race_resolver.rs`'s own module doc, citing `decisions.md §25.2`, states the contract: *"A record's book is the corpus directory it was loaded from... `core_essentials/` appears only inside `RaceChassisRecord::source_path`, where it is the genuine read location, **and never as attribution**."* The contract is violated. `ls data/corpus/core_rulebook/race/` → 7 shipped Core Rulebook race records (dwarf, elf, gnome, half_elf, half_orc, halfling, human); `python3 -c "import json; d=json.load(open('docs/work-inventory.json')); print(sum(1 for u in d['units'] if u.get('book')=='core_rulebook' and u.get('kind')=='race'))"` → **0**. They are labelled `core_essentials:race:*`. `core_essentials` is a PCGen **packaging** directory (`SD-18 decisions.md`: the CRB subtree carries 35 LST files in `core_rulebook/`, 40 in `core_essentials/`, plus ~60 per-race LST files under `core_essentials/races/<race>/`), not a book. That one bucket holds **51 races from many different books** — 7 CRB, ~37 Advanced Race Guide (aasimar, catfolk, changeling, dhampir, drow, duergar, fetchling, gathlain, ghoran, gillman, goblin, grippli, hobgoblin, ifrit, kitsune, kobold, merfolk, nagaji, orc, oread, ratfolk, rougarou, samsaran, skinwalker, strix, suli, svirfneblin, sylph, syrinx, tengu, tiefling, undine, vanara, vishkanya, wayang, wyrwood, wyvaran), plus android/kasatha/lashunta/triaxian from elsewhere — so **`advanced_race_guide` reports exactly ONE race unit**. Corpus-wide the same shape is large: `python3 -c "import json,sys,collections,os; sys.path.insert(0,'scripts/observer'); import pf1e_dashboard_producer as P; d=json.load(open('docs/work-inventory.json')); U=[u for u in d['units'] if u.get('book') not in P.EXCLUDED_BOOKS]; bad=collections.Counter(); n=0\nfor u in U:\n b,k=u.get('book'),u.get('kind')\n if b and k and not os.path.isdir(f'data/corpus/{b}/{k}') and u.get('status') in ('grounded','text-complete','ingested-magnitude','literal-verified','fixture-verified'): bad[(b,k)]+=1; n+=1\nprint(n, bad.most_common(12))"` → **4,007 units are `grounded` or better while their own `book`+`kind` has no `data/corpus/<book>/<kind>/` directory at all** (core_rulebook/equipment_modifier 676, bestiary/monster 326, ultimate_psionics/equipment 326, bestiary/monster_ability 323, ultimate_combat/feat 231, ...). Every one is unverifiable for a **labelling** reason, not a work reason, and the population maps onto the board's 5,596 `held` units. **Corroborating symptom:** the 6 races wave 2's Epic 1 chassis landed (Fetchling, Grippli, Ifrit, Oread, Sylph, Undine) and wave 3's Skinwalker all still read `status=not-ingested`, verdict `not-started`, under `core_essentials:race:*` — real capability work that could not have been credited even if perfect. **Why no instrument caught it:** every check in the chain agrees *with itself* — the inventory says `core_essentials`, the sweep looks for `core_essentials`, finds nothing, reports "not verified", which is true. Nothing ever asks *"the Core Rulebook has seven races, so why does the board say none?"* That needs outside knowledge of what the books contain, and no gate here holds it; even this program's rank-1 detector (ad-hoc commands over source data) was blind, because the source data was self-consistent. **NOTE — partially addressed, not fixed:** `SD31-E6-F3-002` (wave 4) fixed `corpus_literal_sweep`'s attribution enough to move `race` from 0 → 7 done. But `core_rulebook` still reports **0** race units and `core_essentials` still holds **1,610** units across 8 kinds at this tip, so the *label* defect is untouched and the dashboard the operator reads is still wrong. | Orchestrator recorded the finding with full derivation rather than dispatching a same-wave fix (wave 4 owned the primary checkout when it was raised). Dispatched as **wave 5's lead card**: assert the documented contract mechanically as a gate proven able to fail; re-attribute the 1,610-unit `core_essentials` bucket one record deep against the corpus so `core_essentials` ends with zero units; re-run `corpus_literal_sweep` and report how many of the 4,007 reach `literal-verified`/`done`; fix the dashboard's per-book panel if it needs the same correction. **This is a relabel, the exact shape Decision 1(a) forbids doing carelessly** — each unit's new book must be proven from where its record actually lives, with an Opus verifier attacking on precisely that basis. | *(no ruling needed — this is a defect to fix, recorded here because the operator raised it and will want to see it closed)* |

Two distinct shapes: **(A)** a flat (non-scaling) numeric value stated only in an `SPROP:`/`BENEFIT:` field — `advanced_players_guide:equipment_modifier:special_ability_corrosive_weapon`'s only content is `SPROP:+1d6 acid damage`, already `status: text-complete`/`done` on the live board BEFORE this cycle (confirmed pre-existing, not introduced by this cycle); same shape in `mythic_adventures:feat:mythic_feat_output_elemental_fist` (`BENEFIT:` "increases to 1d8 points... %1 additional uses...per day"), `monster_codex:feat:coordinated_reposition` (`BENEFIT:` "move 5 feet"), `bestiary_2:monster_ability:devilfish_water_dependency` ("1 hour"/"2 hours"), `bestiary_4:monster_ability:colossus_pinning_stomp` ("twice that of its slam attack"), `core_rulebook:companion:strength_damage` ("1d6 points of Strength damage"), `ultimate_wilderness:companion:pufferfish_spines` ("1 point of piercing damage"), `core_rulebook:equipment:crossbow_heavy` ("-4 penalty", situational/conditional). **(B)** `wiring_class: static` specifically missing a Strength-bonus-scaled damage formula phrased without a "per level"-style trigger phrase: `core_essentials:race_trait:dragon_claw`/`dragon_tail_slap`/`dragon_wing`, each stating "deal[s] the indicated damage plus [a fraction of] the dragon's Strength bonus" — all 3 are `status: not-ingested` (no current over-claim). **Open interpretive question, not resolved here:** every one of the 9 states a *flat* value, never a *character-specific scaling* formula — `wiring_class::determine_closure`'s own `prose_scaling_phrases` design already treats "scales with a formula" as the bar for "something to compute", distinct from "a flat number is printed in the text". Whether Decision 7's "nothing to compute" means (a) no numeric value appears at all, or (b) no *character-specific* computation is owed, decides whether these 9 (and the unknown-sized wider population they sample) are really proxy failures or not — an operator-level call, not this cycle's to make. Full methodology, the complete 121-unit hand-labelled evidence file, and the regex pre-screen's own disclosed blind spot (it missed 2 of these 9 that the hand read still caught) are at `artifacts/SD31-D7-PROSE-001-proxy-validation.md` and `artifacts/SD31-D7-PROSE-001-proxy-sample-evidence.json`. | Reported precisely, with every record quoted and re-derivable, rather than either (a) silently trusting the proxy at the scale Decision 7's own ruling authorizes, or (b) unilaterally tightening `wiring_class.rs`'s prose-magnitude scan (out of this card's file territory — owned by a sibling this wave — and a real doctrine call about what "nothing to compute" means, not a bug fix). Confirmed the 9 findings do not affect anything this cycle shipped: the new `race_trait` rung and the description-completeness fix both operate strictly on TOP of the existing `wiring_class == 'display'` gate and never touch wiring-class assignment. | *(does Decision 7's "nothing to compute" mean no numeric value at all, or no character-specific scaling formula? Resolving this either confirms the `corrosive_weapon`-shaped 9 (and however many more exist corpus-wide) as real over-claims needing a `wiring_class.rs` fix, or confirms `wiring_class.rs`'s existing scaling-only bar is already correct and these 9 are fine as `display`/`done`. Either way, `wiring_class.rs` is a sibling's file this wave — not for this cycle to change unilaterally.)* |
| 70 | 2026-08-16 | SD31-D7-PROSE-001 | NOTE | `closure_has_real_description()` (new this cycle, `src/bin/v06_work_inventory.rs`) reads the raw `.lst` closure (base row + same-KEY `.MOD` rows) for a `DESC:` token — it does NOT consult the already-ingested corpus JSON's `data.description` field, which for a `.COPY=` equipment/equipment_modifier record can carry a REAL description the ingest pipeline resolved by inheriting from the record's base item (a fact the raw `.lst` closure alone cannot see, since the inheritance resolution happens at ingest time, not in the `.lst` text). This is a **false-negative, the safe direction** (no unit is wrongly promoted; some are conservatively left `unmeasurable` instead of `done`) — deliberately not chased further this cycle given the scope already delivered; a future cycle can recover these 247 by having `closure_has_real_description` (or its caller) also consult the corpus JSON's `data.description` as a second source, keyed by the same `(source_file, source_line)` coordinate already used for the `.lst` join. | Shipped the conservative (under-claiming) version rather than the more accurate one, consistent with the standing rule that under-claiming is the safe failure direction and over-claiming is the one this program exists to prevent. Quantified the gap precisely rather than leaving it undiscovered. | *(no ruling needed — a well-scoped follow-up, not a decision; the exact recovery mechanism is named above)* |

| 168 | 2026-08-17 | SD31-E4-F2-001 | NOTE | **Found while tracing why row 167's new grounding moved zero board units: at least 13 of the 28 `CLASS_FEATURE_POOLS` entries in `v06_work_inventory.rs` (`src/bin/v06_work_inventory.rs:6183`) can never match ANY real corpus record — a pre-existing defect, not introduced this cycle.** The table's `group` field is matched by exact string equality (`*g == group`, lines 6335/6409) against `corpus_key.split(" ~ ").next()`, but the registered names are the bare pool word (`"Mystery"`, `"Domain"`, `"Bloodline"`, `"Hex"`, `"Curse"`, `"Spirit"`, `"Order"`, `"Blessing"`, `"Revelation"`, `"Animal Focus"`, `"Arcane School"`, `"Grand Discovery"`, `"Advanced Talents"`) while every real corpus group is `"<Adjective/Class> <PoolWord>"` (`"Battle Mystery"`, `"Air Domain"`, `"Aberrant Bloodline"`, `"Witch Hex"`, `"Hunter Animal Focus"`, ...) — confirmed for all 13 by direct corpus scan, zero false positives. Sized precisely: **1,562 not-done `class_feature` units** sit under a group whose adjective+word shape can never satisfy the exact-match check (command, groups without a real `" ~ "` separator excluded, one word matched per unit -- first suffix match wins, no double count: `python3 -c "import json,collections; d=json.load(open('docs/work-inventory.json')); U=[u for u in d['units'] if u.get('kind')=='class_feature' and u.get('status') in ('not-ingested','not-started','unknown')]; dead=['Grand Discovery','Advanced Talents','Hex','Revelation','Blessing','Bloodline','Domain','Order','Mystery','Curse','Spirit','Animal Focus','Arcane School']; groups=[(u.get('corpus_key') or '').split(' ~ ')[0] for u in U if ' ~ ' in (u.get('corpus_key') or '')]; c=collections.Counter(next((w for w in dead if g!=w and g.endswith(' '+w)), None) for g in groups); del c[None]; print(sum(c.values()), c.most_common())"` -> 1562 total; by word: Bloodline 452, Domain 422, Mystery 234, Hex 220, Spirit 88, Blessing 82, Animal Focus 35, Arcane School 17, Order 9, Revelation 3). Independently reproduced with a second, unrelated implementation (`awk` over a plain corpus_key-group text dump, not the Python `dict`/JSON path) -- both agree exactly at 1562, per this program's own two-independent-implementations discipline for a board-sizing figure. This means row 152's own "472 units (5.6%) already touch a wired pool" figure is an OVER-count of what the registry can structurally reach today — several of those 28 "covered" pools were never reachable at all, registry aside. | Characterized and quantified; did not fix (`v06_work_inventory.rs` is lane 2's file, out of this card's territory — the same boundary row 167 respected). | *(needs a lane-2 dispatch: change the 13 dead entries' `group` matching from exact-equality to a suffix/pattern match — e.g. `group.ends_with(&format!(" {g}")) \|\| group == *g` — and re-derive the reachable population; 1,562 units is the sizing floor for that fix alone, before any new primitive wiring)* |

(2) Registering the variant exposed a SEPARATE, PRE-EXISTING latent defect in the diagnostic-quote branch, present since before this cycle and not previously reachable: `id.contains(&feature_slug)` on the WHOLE diagnostic id false-matched `"Unchained Rogue Talent ~ Feat"` (`feature_slug == "feat"`) to an unrelated diagnostic (`class_feature.pu.unchained_rogue.corpus_record.improved_uncanny_dodge.unsupported`) purely because every id in this engine begins with the literal token `"class_feature"`, which contains `"feat"` as a substring of `"feature"` — `BTreeMap` alphabetical iteration then picked that wrong diagnostic over the class's own real catch-all purely by sort accident. Fixed via a new `diagnostic_id_names_feature()` that strips only the fixed `"class_feature"` prefix token before searching — narrower than an exact-segment match, which would have regressed established, already-shipped matches this file has never gamed (e.g. `"Bloodrager ~ Bloodrage"` legitimately matches its class's catch-all only because `"bloodrage"` is a substring of the OWNER token `"bloodrager"` itself; `"Slayer Talent ~ Feat"`/`"Ninja Trick ~ Feat"` legitimately match because `"features"` genuinely contains `"feat"` — neither substring lives inside the stripped prefix, both keep working). Checked the fix against all 35 pre-existing `class_feature` `deferred-with-reason` units and found it is **not** a pure no-op there: 34 of 35 are unaffected, but `"Phrenic Slayer ~ AS"` (`feature_slug == "as"`) LOSES its match — its old match was itself the identical spurious-substring shape (`"as"` is a substring of the fixed prefix's own `"class"` token, `cl-AS-s`), not a deliberate one, and `"Phrenic Slayer"` is itself an archetype-qualified group (not the bare `"Slayer"` class), so attributing Slayer's base-class catch-all diagnostic to it was independently already in tension with the `decisions.md §10` AMENDMENT the diagnostic-quote branch has no `group == owner` guard against at all (a broader, separate, NOT-fixed-here gap, flagged for a future cycle). Reported here rather than silently absorbed into "zero regressions" — this fix does not gain-only. **The same universal `"class_feature"`-contains-`"feat"`-or-`"as"` false-positive shape can recur for any future class whose diagnostics happen to sort a spurious match before (or instead of) its own catch-all — the fix is general (strips the prefix for every owner, not only PU), so it will not need re-discovering per class, but the diagnostic-quote branch's total absence of a `group == owner` guard remains a standing, unfixed gap this cycle did not have scope to close.** Measured, guarded regen (before/after every unit individually diffed by id, `docs/work-inventory.json` restored per the wave rule): **85** total `class_feature` moves, zero moves in any other `kind` — `not-started`→`done` 45, `unmeasurable`→`not-started` 21, `not-started`→`held` 12, `not-started`→`deferred` 4, `unmeasurable`→`deferred` 1 (`"Unchained Rogue Talent ~ Feat"`, now correctly citing its own class's catch-all), `deferred`→`done` 1, **`deferred`→`not-started` 1** (`"Phrenic Slayer ~ AS"`, the regression above). | 174 | 2026-08-17 | SD31-E6-F10-002 | NOTE | **Refines `OPEN-ISSUES.md` row 128's `description`-vs-`DESC:` coverage-gap finding with a root cause, and explains why the extension named there (row 91/103's remaining scope) is NOT safely buildable as a same-cycle add-on — confirms row 103's own caution rather than contradicting it.** Dry-run analysis (no code committed, no gate touched) over every shipped `lst_token` record carrying both a literal `DESC:` token and a `data.description` field: **16,289 corpus-wide, 2,594 (16%) byte-mismatch** (independently re-derives row 128's 2,186/16,817 figure, same shape, different snapshot). Traced the FIRST several mismatches by hand (`data/corpus/core_rulebook/equipment/arms_armor/net_base.json` et al.) and found the root cause: PCGen's `.lst` `DESC:` fields encode a literal newline as `&nl;`, and the shipped `description` field correctly DECODES it to a real `\n` for player display — a deliberate, correct transformation, not a defect. Re-ran the same comparison normalizing `&nl;` -> `\n` first: mismatch count barely moves (2,594 -> 2,404 corpus-wide), and the residual is NOT uniform either — a second sample (`occult_adventures/class_feature/medium/*`) shows the OPPOSITE convention: `description` there still carries the raw, UNDECODED `&nl;` literal. **Confirmed empirically: different kinds/ingest paths handle PCGen's escape-entity encoding inconsistently, exactly the cross-kind risk row 103 named** ("a wrong reconstruction rule would itself manufacture false findings across other kinds' differently-shaped description conventions"). Equipment-specific slice (my own file territory): 5,780 `equipment*`-dir records, 1,803 with both fields, **249 mismatch even after `&nl;` normalization is applied only to the corpus-token side** (not separately checked for the reverse convention). A byte-equality `description<->DESC:` gate built today would fire on this genuine, multi-shape encoding variance as if every one were a transcription defect, which is a false-positive explosion, not a working check — building it un-tuned per this card's own instruction would ship a broken gate, and tuning it to swallow the false positives risks masking the real ones row 128 exists to catch. | Did not implement the extension. Quantified the blast radius and identified the specific confound (PCGen entity-encoding conventions varying by ingest path) that a future dedicated TDD pass needs to resolve BEFORE the check can be built, rather than attempting a rushed, likely-wrong version. | *(no ruling needed — an engineering remedy, refining row 128's own: build a SHARED PCGen-entity-decode normalizer (`&nl;`, and audit for siblings like `&comma;`/`&amp;` if present) first, confirm per-kind which convention (raw vs. decoded) each ingest path's `description` field actually uses BEFORE writing the comparison, then extend `compare_tokens` — do not add the check before that survey exists)* |
